Built a new, real `/analytics` page backed by a new shared aggregation service (`backend/services/analyticsService.js`) — every number/chart point is computed from real Firestore report data, never fabricated (Golden Rule #1: an absent metric like average completion time renders "not enough data," never a fake 0). **Scope decision, enforced entirely server-side per Phase 14's role model:** an account with `canViewAllProfiles` (Owner/Admin/Manager) on an **enterprise-tier** account sees organization-wide analytics (aggregated across the whole team roster, plus a per-member "Team Comparison" breakdown); every other caller — restricted team roles (Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/legacy Editor), and the large majority of accounts (every solo starter/professional/agency user, who can never have a team at all) — sees personal-scope analytics limited to their own `uid`, matching the same organization-isolation model Phase 13/14 established (`organizationId = user.teamOwnerId || user.uid`). The `tier === 'enterprise'` check is a free, I/O-free short-circuit specifically so the extra `enterpriseTeams` roster read never runs for the common case (a real, deliberate performance decision, not an oversight). Metrics delivered exactly as specified: Reports Generated, Photos Analyzed, Average Report Completion Time (createdAt→reviewedAt for finalized reports, legacy `completed`/`approved` folded in — same `isReviewed()` definition `reports.js` already uses elsewhere), Reports Per User (organization scope only), Reports By Type, Reports By Status, Monthly Usage (a fixed rolling 12-calendar-month view, independent of the page's own range filter — a deliberate, documented choice, not an inconsistency), Reports Over Time and Photos Processed Over Time (gap-free daily/weekly/monthly buckets depending on the selected range's width, so a chart never renders thousands of daily bars for an "All Time" view), and Team Usage/team-comparison (the same org-wide Reports Over Time data plus the new Reports Per User breakdown — deliberately not a 5th, redundant dataset). Date-range filtering supports 7/30/90/365-day presets, "All Time," and an explicit custom start/end picker; **timezone/date-boundary accuracy** is handled by having the frontend send real UTC ISO instants (local-midnight-to-local-midnight, computed via plain `Date` local-time semantics, so the browser's own timezone does the conversion correctly with zero IANA-timezone-library dependency) plus a `tzOffset` minutes value on every request, which the backend uses to shift timestamps before reading calendar day/week/month boundaries for chart bucketing — verified with a dedicated unit test at a UTC-day-boundary-crossing instant. **Consolidation decision (the phase's own task 4, "avoid duplicating aggregation logic"):** deliberately did NOT refactor `crmService.js`'s already-shipped, already-tested `buildDashboardAnalytics` or `reports.js`'s `dashboard-summary` endpoint to call into the new service — both are already live-QA'd and covered by existing tests asserting their exact output, and `crmService.js`'s turnaround-hours definition subtly excludes legacy `completed` status while the new, more-complete `isReviewed()`-aligned definition includes it; forcing a shared implementation would have silently changed already-shipped production behavior for any account with legacy data, which is out of this phase's scope. The new module mirrors the same *pattern* (pure, dependency-free aggregator functions + a thin Firestore-fetching wrapper, unit-tested exactly like `crm-analytics.test.js` already is) so a real future consolidation stays a straightforward refactor, documented in `analyticsService.js`'s own header comment rather than silently skipped. **A real bug caught by its own unit test before shipping:** the timezone-shifting helper's sign convention was inverted (`date - tzOffsetMinutes*60000` instead of `date + tzOffsetMinutes*60000`), which would have bucketed every report into the wrong local day for any non-UTC caller — caught by a dedicated negative-offset boundary-crossing test, fixed, and the test's own fixture (which had also encoded an inconsistent assumption) corrected alongside it. Frontend: new `frontend/src/pages/Analytics.jsx` (standalone route ahead of the Phase 30 routing migration, same precedent as `Templates.jsx`/`TeamMemberProfile.jsx`) with range-filter chips + custom date pickers, 3 headline stat cards, 2 breakdown bar-charts (By Type/By Status), a 12-month usage bar chart, 2 scrollable time-series bar charts, and a conditional Team Comparison section — all built with the same plain-Tailwind-bars charting style `CRM.jsx`'s existing dashboard already uses (no new chart-library dependency added; `chart.js`/`react-chartjs-2` remain the pre-existing, documented, zero-import dead dependencies they already were). Added a "Analytics" nav entry (new `href`-based sidebar item, matching the existing Templates-link pattern) to both `Dashboard.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s sidebars, available to every tier (this is the *general* analytics page, not an enterprise-only feature) — mirrors the existing precedent that in-app authenticated pages get sidebar entries, not top-`Navbar` links. Backend `npm test` → **171/171 passing** (12 new, `analytics-service.test.js` — pure-function coverage for every aggregation rule including the timezone-boundary case), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 44 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 14 warnings (unchanged or better — no new warnings from this phase's own code), `npm run build` → succeeds (new `Analytics` chunk builds correctly, 10.57kB). **Live-verified against the real Firebase project — no browser automation tool available this session either (same documented gap as Phases 7/13/14).** A throwaway Node script (run from outside `backend/`, avoiding the established nodemon-restart hazard) seeded **7 real throwaway accounts** via the Firebase Admin SDK directly — an organization (`owner1`) with a real Admin, Adjuster, and Viewer team member; a fully separate second organization (`owner2`, to test cross-org isolation); a solo professional-tier account; and a starter-tier account with zero reports ever — plus **9 real report documents with hand-chosen `createdAt`/`reviewedAt`/`status`/`reportType`/`imageCount` values**, authenticated via real signed custom JWTs through `authenticateToken`'s existing fallback path, driving **55/55 checks, all passing** against the live HTTP API (verified under both the legacy `/api` and versioned `/api/v1` prefixes): every headline number (reportsGenerated/photosAnalyzed/avgCompletionHours, including the exact averaged-hours math) matched hand-calculated expected values across the 30-day default range, a 90-day range (correctly picking up an older report), an all-time range (correctly folding a legacy `completed`-status report into the "finalized" bucket), and a custom range narrow enough to isolate exactly one specific report; organization-wide scope correctly triggered only for the Owner (with the right team size and a correct zero-filled-for-non-generating-members Reports-Per-User breakdown); the restricted Adjuster role correctly saw personal-scope-only data (never the org's Admin/Owner reports) despite being a member of the same organization; the solo professional account and the enterprise-but-restricted-Viewer account both correctly took the personal-scope path (confirming the tier/capability short-circuit works from both directions); the starter-tier zero-report account got an honest all-zero/null response with no crash; **cross-organization isolation was proven in both directions** — `owner2`'s single report never appeared in `owner1`'s org-wide totals, and vice versa; unauthenticated and garbage-token requests correctly got `401`; and a live suspend of the Adjuster team member proved the exact same defense-in-depth mechanics Phase 14 built — a token minted *after* suspension (fresh `tokenVersion`) still got `403 TEAM_ACCESS_SUSPENDED` from the live per-request status check, while the pre-suspension stale token got `401 TOKEN_REVOKED` from the version check — both independently confirmed working against this new route. All 7 accounts, their 9 reports, and their 3 `enterpriseTeams` membership docs were fully deleted afterward via a dedicated cleanup script, re-verified with a zero-residue query pass; `git status` shows only the expected new/modified files. **Not covered live:** as with every prior phase in this environment, `Analytics.jsx`'s actual rendering/chart-scrolling/mobile-responsiveness/tooltip behavior was verified by code review + a successful production build only, since no browser automation tool was available — every underlying number and permission boundary the page displays was independently verified live and directly at the HTTP level, as detailed above. **Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything. Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 16 or any further phase.**
- **Previous:** — 2026-08-18: Implemented **Phase 14 only** (Team Roles Expansion & Member Profiles) per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 14 only"), following session 18's Phase 13 completion. **Before any code was written**, per the client's own explicit instruction, a least-privilege permissions-matrix proposal was presented and stopped for approval — the client approved the matrix as proposed, plus 4 named decisions (legacy `editor` role kept, never silently migrated; all 4 suspension safeguards; team roster stays inside `EnterpriseDashboard`, only the member-profile view becomes a standalone route; organization-template management becomes an explicit Owner/Admin/Manager allow-list) and a full anti-lockout/anti-escalation rule set (no self-role-change/self-suspend/self-remove; only Owner assigns Admin; Admin cannot modify Owner or other Admins; Manager can only manage Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/legacy-Editor and cannot assign Manager/Admin/Owner; ownership transfer explicitly out of scope, not simulated). See `PHASES.md` Phase 14 for the full approved capability matrix table. New shared `backend/utils/orgRoles.js` became the single source of truth for the 7-role (+ legacy `editor`) capability matrix and hierarchy, consumed by a rewritten `backend/routes/teams.js`, new `requireTeamCapability` gates on 8 routes in `backend/routes/reports.js` (`POST /generate`/`/:id/analysis/retry`/`/:id/photos/regenerate`/`/:id/images` require `canGenerate`; `POST /:id/approve` requires `canApprove`; `POST /:id/export` + `GET /:id/download` require `canExport`; `PUT /:id` + both `/sections/*` AI-assist routes + the photo-review endpoint require `canEditReports`, with an added narrow rule that a role lacking `canApprove` — i.e. Inspector — can only edit a report *before* it's been reviewed), and `backend/services/templateService.js` (Decision #4: `canManageOrganizationTemplates` now delegates to `hasCapability(user, 'canManageTemplates')`, an explicit Owner/Admin/Manager allow-list, replacing the old "anything that isn't a viewer" rule). **Three real, pre-existing bugs found and fixed while making the roles real (not new regressions — they predate this phase, surfaced by actually implementing role-based management for the first time):** (1) `GET /teams/members` and every mutation endpoint matched only the literal account owner's own uid — no role, including the old 4-role model's "Admin," could ever actually manage the team before this phase; a non-owner login saw a silently empty/broken roster. (2) `PUT /members/:id/role` only ever updated the `enterpriseTeams` row, never the target member's own `users/{uid}` doc — a role change had literally never taken effect for that member's own live permission checks. (3) `DELETE /members/:id` (remove) only deleted the roster row and left the removed member's own account permanently enterprise-tier forever; it now reverts `teamOwnerId`/`teamRole`/`teamPermissions`/`teamMemberId`/`teamMembershipStatus`/`tier` on their own account and bumps their `tokenVersion` + calls `revokeRefreshTokens()`. **Suspension ships with all 4 requested safeguards:** an immediate `status: 'suspended'` flip (member stays visible to admins, never hidden, satisfying the acceptance criteria directly), a `tokenVersion` bump (kills outstanding custom JWTs immediately), a new `isTeamMembershipSuspended()` check in `backend/middleware/auth.js` wired into every `req.user`-setting code path (`authenticateToken`'s Firebase-ID-token AND custom-JWT branches, `authenticateApiKey`, `optionalAuth`) — this is the actual "live per-request check," and it costs zero extra Firestore reads since it just reads a field off the user doc every authenticated request already fetches — and a real `getAuth().revokeRefreshTokens(uid)` call, confirmed live via the real Firebase Auth user record's `tokensValidAfterTime` genuinely advancing. Reactivation clears the flags and deliberately does *not* re-issue/re-revoke anything further (suspend already forced a fresh login; nothing stale can be resurrected). **A real, flagged architectural limitation, not hidden:** since reports have always been strictly `userId`-scoped (no shared team report pool exists — that's Phase 19's job) and the approved matrix gives Inspector `canApprove: false`, a report an Inspector generates can never actually be approved by anyone at all today — not the Inspector (blocked by role) and not anyone else (they don't own it). Left unfixed as explicitly out of this phase's scope; Phase 19's eventual cross-member review-request workflow is the real fix. **Frontend:** `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s Team tab is now role-aware — the role guide/invite dropdown/role-change dropdown are all driven live by `GET /teams/roles`'s `assignableRoles` (previously 3 hardcoded roles with a hand-maintained, silently-staleable permissions list), the hardcoded "Owner row" now shows the real org owner's info instead of assuming the viewer always IS the owner (a real, previously-silent bug: any enterprise-tier login could reach `/enterprise-dashboard`, not just the owner), members lacking `canManageTeam`/`canViewAllProfiles` now see an honest "Team management is limited to Owners, Admins, and Managers" card instead of a broken empty roster, and row actions are shown per-member only when the server-provided `manageableTargetRoles` actually allows it (hierarchy consumed, never re-implemented client-side). New standalone `frontend/src/pages/TeamMemberProfile.jsx` at `/team/members/:memberId` (`me` supported for self-lookup, added to `App.jsx` ahead of the Phase 30 routing migration, same precedent as `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`/`Templates.jsx`) shows real name/email/role(+legacy note)/status, real Reports-Generated/Photos-Analyzed stat tiles (same `.select()`-projection-summed-in-Node pattern as the existing `dashboard-summary` endpoint, avoiding a Firestore composite index this project has never provisioned), a real recent-activity list sourced from the existing `auditLogs` collection, and — only when the server says `canManage: true` for that specific viewer/target pair — admin actions (role change/suspend-reactivate/remove, the last behind the existing shared `ConfirmDialog`). Backend `npm test` → **159/159 passing** (unchanged — the existing `teams.test.js`/`template-service.test.js` suites already covered the relocated/extended pure-logic helpers and needed zero test changes), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 44 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline — 2 warnings introduced mid-session by this phase's own new code, `membersErrorCode`/an unused `Check` icon import, were found and fixed before the final count), `npm run build` → succeeds (new `TeamMemberProfile` chunk builds correctly). **Live-verified against the real Firebase project — no browser automation tool available this session either (same documented gap as Phases 7 and 13; no `chromium-cli`/Playwright installed).** Two throwaway Node scripts, run from outside `backend/` (avoiding the same nodemon-restart-mid-job hazard Phase 13 first documented), drove **10 real throwaway accounts** — an organization (`owner1`) with a real invited Admin, 2 Managers, an Adjuster, an Inspector, a Reviewer, a Viewer, and a second Admin (to test admin-vs-admin hierarchy), a manufactured pre-Phase-14-shaped legacy Editor (deliberately created without the new `teamMemberId`/`teamMembershipStatus` fields, to prove the self-healing suspend path), plus a fully separate second organization and one entirely unrelated outsider — authenticated via real signed custom JWTs through `authenticateToken`'s existing fallback path (real Firebase Auth users were still created for every account specifically so `revokeRefreshTokens()` had a real record to act on) — through **87/87 checks, all passing**, split across two runs by a self-inflicted local rate-limiter (100 req/15min) trip: unlike every prior phase's session, this environment's safety classifier correctly refused to let the dev-server process (which this session didn't start) be killed to clear the limiter's in-memory store, so the run was simply split at a `--from-j` checkpoint and re-run after waiting the 15-minute window out twice instead — a testing-infrastructure fact, not a product bug, and required no code change. Every one of the 8 roles' capability flags matched the approved matrix exactly via `GET /teams/roles`; roster visibility correctly split Owner/Admin/Manager (200) from Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer (403 `TEAM_LIST_DENIED`); every role's own `GET /members/me` returned its own real record; profile-view authorization correctly distinguished self-view/`canViewAllProfiles`-view/denied with a per-pair-correct `canManage` flag; the full anti-escalation suite passed (Admin blocked from assigning/modifying Admin, Manager blocked from assigning/modifying Manager or Admin, nobody can invite/assign `owner` or `editor`, nobody can self-modify); a live proof confirmed a never-reissued JWT loses `canManageTeam` the instant the owner changes that account's role, zero token invalidation involved; suspension was proven immediate through 3 independent mechanisms (stale-tokenVersion `401`, fresh-tokenVersion-but-still-suspended `403` proving the live status check specifically, and real `tokensValidAfterTime` advancement); the suspended record stayed visible/queryable throughout; reactivation restored access cleanly; removal genuinely reverted the account to solo starter-tier (tier-gated back out of every team route) rather than a broken suspended-forever state; a second organization's owner got clean `404`s on every attempt to touch org1's members and saw a genuinely empty roster of their own; report-action gating was confirmed for Inspector (generate ✅, edit-draft ✅, approve/export ❌, including the specific pre-approval-edit rule against a manually-finalized report), Reviewer (generate ❌), and a full real Adjuster generate→wait-for-pipeline→approve→export lifecycle (a real PDF was genuinely exported to Storage); the legacy Editor generated/approved a real report with Adjuster-equivalent capabilities, was suspended/reactivated through the self-healing mirrored-field path, and was explicitly migrated to `adjuster` by an admin (never silently); and real `auditLogs` entries were confirmed for every invite/role-change/suspend/reactivate/remove action, with a member's own profile `recentActivity`/`reportsGenerated` stats confirmed to reflect genuinely real, freshly-generated data. All 10 accounts, their reports (4), one organization template, and one exported PDF Storage object were fully deleted afterward via a dedicated cleanup script (re-run once more at the end, confirming 0 residual QA records) plus a separate Storage-specific cleanup pass; `git status` confirms zero repo residue (only the two new files — `backend/utils/orgRoles.js`, `frontend/src/pages/TeamMemberProfile.jsx` — plus the expected modified files appear). **Not covered live:** as with Phases 7 and 13, `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s role-aware Team tab and the new `TeamMemberProfile.jsx` page's actual rendering/loading/empty/error states/mobile responsiveness were verified by code review + a successful production build only, since no browser automation tool was available — every underlying API call and permission boundary those pages exercise was independently verified live and directly at the HTTP level, as detailed above. True concurrent-request races were not tested. Ownership transfer was, per explicit client instruction, neither implemented nor simulated. **Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything. Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 15 or any further phase.**
- **Previous:** — 2026-08-18: Implemented **Phase 13 only** (Real Template Builder) per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 13 only"), following session 17's Phase 12 completion. Built a new, real structural template system — new `templates` Firestore collection (`backend/services/templateService.js`) covering name/description/wizard-field defaults/a template-chosen `requiredFields` subset/Phase-9-shaped custom `sections`/Phase-11-shaped `photoLayout` export defaults/`branding` (companyName/footerText/Storage-backed logo) — across `personal`/`organization`/`flacron` scopes, alongside (not replacing) the pre-existing shallow `reportTemplates` collection/`/reports/templates` endpoints, which remain fully untouched for backward compatibility. This codebase has no separate "organizations" collection, so "organization" scope reuses the existing enterprise-team model (`teams.js`'s `teamOwnerId`/`teamRole`): `organizationId = user.teamOwnerId || user.uid`, so a solo account's org is just itself and organization templates degrade gracefully until that account has teammates. Real Create/Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Restore/Delete landed via new `backend/routes/templates.js` (mounted `/api/templates`, browser-only) with server-side `canView`/`canEdit` enforcing scope rules: personal templates are owner-only; organization templates are visible to every same-org member and editable by any non-`viewer` role; Flacron templates are visible to everyone and never editable/deletable by anyone (`403`, not a silent no-op). Duplicate always produces an owned personal copy regardless of the source's scope (including duplicating a Flacron template as an editable starting point) and never inherits the source's Storage-backed logo. **A real IDOR-shaped risk was closed by design, not found after the fact:** the general template-edit endpoint deliberately never accepts a client-supplied `logoObjectPath`/`logoUrl` — only the dedicated `POST /:id/logo` upload route (which controls the actual Storage write) can set them, since otherwise a client could point a template's "logo" at an arbitrary Storage path it doesn't own (e.g. another user's private report photo) and have it silently downloaded into every export generated from that template. Seeded 6 real Flacron example templates exactly as named in `PHASES.md` (Residential Property, Water Loss, Roof, Wind/Hail, Fire, Commercial Property), each with genuine cautious-language custom sections, seeded idempotently and lazily on first list call. New standalone `/templates` (My/Organization/Flacron tabs, Create/Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Restore/Delete) + `/templates/new`/`/templates/:id/edit` (full builder: Basics/Default Wording/Required Fields/Additional Report Sections with add-reorder-remove/Photo & Export Layout/Branding Defaults with logo upload) routes, added ahead of the Phase 30 routing migration — same precedent as `ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (Phase 11). Wired a new `TemplatePickerModal` into the wizard's new "Start From a Report Template" step in `Dashboard.jsx`: selecting a template applies its `fields` to the existing `form` state plus a new `templateId` (already flows through the wizard's existing generic FormData submission with zero new plumbing). `POST /generate` resolves `templateId` server-side (never trusts client-supplied structure), enforces the template's `requiredFields` (`400 TEMPLATE_REQUIRED_FIELD_MISSING` with the exact missing-field list, now surfaced as a real toast instead of the generic "Generation failed" fallback), and persists `templateId`/`templateName`/`templateGuidance`/`templateSections`/`exportDefaults`/`templateBranding` onto the new report. **A template's custom sections are deterministically appended to the AI-generated content** (new `appendTemplateSections()` in `richContent.js`) rather than left to prompt luck — the same "don't trust the LLM alone" pattern Phase 8 established for photo observations — and both `photoJobService.retryFailedAnalysis`/`regenerateFromPhotoReview` re-read the persisted template fields so a retry or photo-review regenerate re-applies the same structure instead of silently losing it. A template's `photoLayout` seeds `ExportOptionsModal`'s initial checkbox/layout state via the new `report.exportDefaults` field (zero call-site changes needed, since the modal already receives the full `report` object). **A second real bug found and fixed during live testing:** `POST /:id/export`'s new `templateBranding` fallback for `companyName`/`reportFooter` resolved correctly into the export options object but never actually rendered, because the PDF/DOCX/HTML generators only ever display custom branding text when `hideFlacronBranding` is true (normally an enterprise white-label-only switch) — fixed with a narrowly-scoped additional fallback (`!userData.company && !!templateBranding?.companyName`) that only engages when there's no white-label config and no personal company name to defer to instead, verified live across HTML and DOCX (PDF verified by code-path inspection, sharing the identical resolution logic). **A pre-existing, unrelated gap found and flagged, not fixed:** non-enterprise users' own Settings → Profile "Company Name" field has the identical silent-inert-ness bug just described — predates Phase 13 entirely, left untouched to avoid scope creep into an already-shipped, unrelated feature. Backend `npm test` → **159/159 passing** (24 new, `template-service.test.js` — access-control matrix, sanitizers, the IDOR guard, `appendTemplateSections`, Flacron seed-data coverage), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 44 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline), `npm run build` → succeeds (new `Templates`/`TemplateBuilder` chunks build correctly). **Live-verified at the API level against the real Firestore project and real Anthropic API — no browser automation tool (`chromium-cli`/Playwright) was available in this session's environment, and installing one unprompted was judged disproportionate; this is a real gap relative to Phases 8-12's Playwright-based sessions.** Two throwaway Node scripts drove the real running HTTP API directly against 5 real throwaway accounts (an organization owner + editor + viewer sharing one organization, plus 2 fully unrelated outsiders), authenticated via real signed custom JWTs through the existing `authenticateToken` fallback path, all fully deleted afterward: **49/49 checks passed** — all 6 Flacron templates seeded and visible to everyone; personal-template owner-only visibility, invisible to outsiders; organization-template same-org visibility, invisible to outsiders; role enforcement (viewer → `403` edit, editor → `200`, outsider → `403`/`404`); Flacron immutability (edit/delete both `403` for every account); Duplicate always produces an owned personal copy; Archive/Restore; required-field validation (missing → `400` naming the exact field, present → `201` with `exportDefaults`/`templateBranding` correctly reflecting the template); a real end-to-end Claude-API generation (~90s) confirming the template's custom section appended verbatim; logo upload/removal via a real Storage round-trip with cross-user upload correctly rejected; and the branding-fallback fix confirmed actually rendering in real exported HTML and DOCX (`word/document.xml` inspected directly via `pizzip`). **A real, reproduced testing-methodology hazard, not a Phase 13 defect:** an earlier scratch verification script created inside `backend/` while a background report-generation pipeline was in flight triggered `nodemon`'s file-watcher to restart the server, killing that in-flight job permanently — a real, reproduced instance of Phase 7's own documented "no persistent job queue survives a restart" tradeoff; all further scratch scripts this session were run from outside the repo entirely to avoid this. All test data (5 accounts, 4 templates, 3 reports, their Storage objects) was fully deleted afterward; `git status` confirms zero repo residue. **Not covered live:** the wizard's template picker UI, `/templates`, and the builder pages' actual rendering, loading/empty/error states, and mobile responsiveness were verified by code review + a successful production build only, not by looking at a rendered page or a browser console — recommend a follow-up pass with browser automation available before treating the frontend UI layer as fully QA'd. **Client has instructed: do not commit, push, or deploy anything. Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 14 or any further phase** (recommend a real-browser verification pass for this phase's frontend first).
- **Previous:** — 2026-08-18: Implemented **Phase 12 only** (My Reports & Claims Management Completion) per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 12 only"), following session 16's Phase 11 completion. **My Reports** (`Dashboard.jsx`) gained a collapsible Filters panel (Claim #, Loss Type, Report Type, Creator, Created-date range, Organization — all real server-side query params on `GET /reports`, most newly added: `reportType`, `clientId`, `claimNumber`, `creator`) and a new "More actions" row menu (`RowActionsMenu`, a new local component) adding Duplicate, Download PDF, Share (disabled with a tooltip unless finalized), and Archive-or-Restore, with permanent Delete moved into the same menu; Archive now gets its own non-danger confirm dialog clearly distinct from Delete's danger one, and a new `createdByEmail` field is stored at generate time (real, forward-compatible creator attribution — single-valued per account today since no shared team report pool exists yet, not fabricated). A new `POST /:id/duplicate` copies the complete Phase 5 claim/inspection field set into a clean draft (no photos/documents/content, per the phase's own risk-note recommendation), with a visible " (Copy)" suffix on the claim number. A new `POST /:id/restore` reverses Archive back to a report's exact pre-archive status (draft or finalized, via a new `preArchiveStatus` field) rather than always resetting to draft, protecting a finalized report's clean-export state across an archive/restore round trip. **Claims** (`CRM.jsx`) gained a real search box and loss-date range filter (both server-side via `crmService.getClaims`'s extended params) and Edit/Archive/Restore/Delete row actions — all wired onto the CRM backend's pre-existing `PUT`/`DELETE /claims/:id` endpoints, which had simply never been called from any UI; Archive/Restore reuses the same update endpoint with a new `archived` boolean field (validated server-side), no new route needed; `NewClaimModal` gained an edit mode (pre-fills the form, calls `updateClaim` instead of `createClaim`). **Claim workspace** (`CRMClaimProfile.jsx`) was rebuilt from a single page of stacked sections into a real tabbed layout (Overview/Reports/Photos), with loading/error/retry states added throughout; the new **Photos tab** aggregates real photos across every report linked to the claim (via the existing `GET /reports/:id/photos`), with Report/Category/Review-status filters, and a report whose photos fail to load is skipped rather than failing the whole tab. **The Claims tier-gating open question was deliberately left unresolved, not guessed at:** Phase 12's own 2026-08-16 amendment claimed this question was "added as an Open Question below" in `PHASES.md`, but it was never actually enumerated there — a documentation gap. Per the client's explicit instruction this session to stop and ask before implementing that specific behavior if still unresolved, this phase left Claims exactly as tier-gated as it already was (Agency/Enterprise only) — none of this phase's actual tasks required deciding whether to expose Claims more broadly; the question is now properly logged as Open Question #15 in `PHASES.md` for a future decision. **A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** duplicating a report set the editor's `editableContent` state to the literal JavaScript `null` (a fresh duplicate's `content` field, since Duplicate deliberately never runs AI generation), which `SectionedReportEditor`'s markdown parser (`String(content)`) rendered as the literal text **"null"** inside the editor — a real, visible bug that looked like corrupted data, not a crash. Fixed by falling back to `''` at the one call site that hadn't already followed the rest of the codebase's established null-guard convention. Backend `npm test` → **135/135 passing** (unchanged), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 44 warnings (+1 over the 43 baseline — one new `catch(err)` following the codebase's existing accepted pattern). Frontend `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline), `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged), `npm run build` → succeeds. **Fully live-verified against the real Anthropic API and real Firebase project** (2 throwaway Agency-tier QA accounts plus one seeded CRM client/claim, created via the Firebase Admin SDK and fully deleted afterward along with a real end-to-end generated report and its Storage objects, confirmed zero repo residue via `git status`): **My Reports filters — 13/13** (every filter correctly isolated its expected report(s); Clear Filters restored the full list). **My Reports actions — 15/15 in a final clean consolidated run** (Duplicate → correct claim-number suffix and, post-fix, a clean empty editor; Archive → non-danger dialog, disappears/reappears correctly under the Archived filter with Restore; Restore → back to All as a draft; permanent Delete → gone entirely; Download PDF → a real file downloads; Share → enabled with a real working copied link for a finalized report, disabled with a tooltip for a draft). **Claims tab — 10/10** (search, empty state, date range, Edit persistence, Archive/Restore/Show-Archived). **Claim workspace — 7/7** (direct URL load, all three tabs present, Reports tab shows the linked report(s), Photos tab correctly aggregated 2 real AI-analyzed photos from a genuine end-to-end wizard-generated report linked to the seeded claim with working filters, survives a refresh). **Cross-user authorization — 5/5** (a second account gets a clean "Claim unavailable" with zero data leaked, never sees the first account's reports). **Mobile 390×844 — 5/5** (zero horizontal overflow, zero console/network errors). Several self-inflicted local dev rate-limiter (429) trips from this session's own extensive rapid repeated test iteration were each resolved via a backend restart and confirmed not regressions by clean re-runs — the same well-documented pattern from Phases 9-11; the one pre-existing, unrelated stuck `processing` report from 2026-07-29 remains present and was left untouched. **Not covered live:** a genuinely pre-Phase-6 legacy-shaped report (no `photos[]` array) was not specifically re-tested against the new filters/actions — these operate on fields that exist identically on legacy reports and Duplicate never touches photo fields either way, so no legacy-specific behavior was introduced, but this wasn't independently re-confirmed with a real synthesized legacy record; pagination beyond a single page wasn't exercised. **Client has instructed: do not commit, push, or deploy anything. Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 13 or any further phase.**
- **Previous:** — 2026-08-18: Implemented **Phase 11 only** (Export Options Modal & PDF Layout Completion, expanded scope per the 2026-08-16 amendment to "Report Preview, Export Options & Document Layout Completion") per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 11 only"), following session 15's Phase 10 completion. Built a real, standalone `/reports/:id/preview` route (`frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, added to `App.jsx`) ahead of the full Phase 30 routing migration — same precedent as Phases 9 and 10, since this one page didn't need the whole `activeView` architecture migrated first. It fetches its report independently by ID (`reportsAPI.getOne`), so a direct URL visit or a page refresh both work with zero dependency on `Dashboard.jsx`'s in-memory state; offers a Desktop (via the existing `ReportMarkdown` component) / PDF (lazy-loaded iframe) toggle, and Edit/Approve/Export controls gated on report status (`processing`/`regenerating` disables Approve/Export with a locked button, not a hidden one). Its "Edit" action deep-links back to `/dashboard?openReport=<id>` — a new, narrowly-scoped query-param handler added to `Dashboard.jsx` (not a routing migration) that fetches the report directly and opens it in the existing generate/review view. Built a new shared `frontend/src/components/ExportOptionsModal.jsx` (format picker locked per `tier.exportFormats`; the 5 required checkboxes — cover page/photo captions/page numbers/photo appendix/company branding, all default ON so an old client or the modal's own defaults reproduce today's exact export output; a 1/2/4-per-page photo layout picker shown only when relevant), used by both the new preview page and `Dashboard.jsx` (which kept its old one-click quick-download buttons alongside a new "Customize…" link into the modal). `POST /:id/export` (`backend/routes/reports.js`) now parses and validates these options server-side (non-boolean/missing → default `true`; `photoLayout` clamped to `{1,2,4}`, default `2`) and threads them through a new `buildAppendixPhotoList()` helper that resolves the report's real, Phase-8-reviewed photo data (excluded photos dropped, edited observations substituted) into the appendix — never fabricated, with a legacy (pre-Phase-6) fallback to the flat `imagePaths` list with no invented location/observation. **The most significant thing found this session: a real, pre-existing Golden Rule #4 gap, unrelated to this phase's own task list.** `backend/config/tiers.js` has always defined `tier.exportFormats` (e.g. `['pdf']` for starter tier), but `POST /:id/export` never actually checked it — a starter-tier account could get a clean DOCX/HTML export by calling the API directly, bypassing the UI's own format-gating entirely (client-side-only enforcement). Fixed: the route now returns `403 EXPORT_FORMAT_NOT_ALLOWED` for a disallowed format; verified live with a real captured bearer token (starter tier → 403 for docx/html, 200 for pdf). `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js`/`generateHTML` (in `reports.js`) all gained: an optional cover page (off → the Table of Contents becomes page 1); `includeCompanyBranding` toggling the cover mark + header-bar logo/text; an always-on confidentiality-statement + claim-number footer line (not a checkbox); an independently-togglable "Page N" field; and a real 1/2/4-photos-per-page appendix layout (each cell measures its own caption/area/observation text height first so rows page-break cleanly) replacing the old fixed 2-column image-only grid. **A second real bug found during visual verification (a consistency issue, not a security one):** unchecking "Include company branding" hid the footer's small brand-attribution line in the DOCX and HTML generators but not in the PDF generator (whose footer text was never gated by that option). Standardized all three on the PDF's original behavior — the option only ever touches the cover mark + header-bar logo/text, never the footer attribution (paired with the always-on confidentiality line). fixed by computing the next photos array from the `photos` closure directly and calling both setters as independent top-level statements. **A pre-existing, unrelated gap found and flagged (not fixed, out of scope):** `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s `handleGenerate()` still assumes Phase 7's pre-pipeline synchronous model (treats the immediate `POST /generate` response as the finished report) — a gap from Phase 7 never being retrofitted into that dashboard; - **Previous:** — 2026-08-16: Implemented **Phase 7 only** (Async Photo Analysis Pipeline) per explicit client instruction, using the client-selected **in-process async queue** architecture (no new infra — Firestore itself is the durable job store; accepted tradeoff: in-flight work doesn't survive a Render restart mid-job). `POST /generate` now uploads photos synchronously (fast) then responds immediately, running the actual AI analysis+generation pipeline (new `backend/services/photoJobService.js`) as a background, internally-caught async function in the same process — a new `analysisJobs` Firestore collection (matching spec §15's exact field list) tracks per-photo and per-generation-stage status, exposed via a new `GET /:id/analysis-status` polling endpoint. Added automatic in-batch retry (up to 3 attempts) landing failed photos in a distinct `needs_attention` status, plus a manual `POST /:id/analysis/retry` that re-uses already-uploaded bytes and merges fresh (partial) results with prior successful ones via a new `mergeImageAnalysis()` helper — an early bug where a partial retry would have silently discarded all prior findings was caught and fixed before shipping. Added 409 `REPORT_PROCESSING` guards to `PUT /:id`/`POST /:id/approve`/`POST /:id/export` while a report is mid-pipeline. Frontend: a new FLACRON-ENGINE-branded `activeView === 'analysis'` view in `Dashboard.jsx` (real routes are Phase 30's job) with live polling, and My-Reports rows for `status: 'processing'` reports now resume this same view instead of opening the normal detail modal. **Found and fixed a real, load-bearing performance defect during this phase's own live verification:** photo uploads inside `processPhotoBatch` ran fully sequentially (3 Storage round-trips per file), measuring **28.7 seconds** for a 12-photo `POST /generate` — directly undermining this phase's core "client never waits" promise. Fixed with a two-phase restructure (fast sequential validation/dedup-hash pass, then a concurrency-capped worker pool for the actual Storage I/O, `UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY = 8`) — response time dropped to **~9-11 seconds** (3 separate live measurements: 11022ms/9589ms/9061ms), a 61-68% reduction. Backend **94/94 tests passing** (6 new: 5 for `mergeImageAnalysis`, plus rewritten upload-failure tests in `photo-batch-processor.test.js` that now identify target files by content instead of call-index, since concurrent uploads make raw ordering non-deterministic), frontend 4/4, both packages lint-clean and build clean. **Live-verified against the real Firebase project and real Anthropic API** via a throwaway QA account (fully deleted afterward — reports, `analysisJobs`, Firestore user doc, Firebase Auth user; confirmed zero residue via `git status`): 12-photo and 24-photo (3 concurrent vision batches) full pipeline runs both completed correctly with no lost updates or duplicate records; the retry mechanism was exercised end-to-end by injecting a real `needs_attention` state and confirming the merge-not-replace fix holds (analyzed count increased, not reset); all three 409 guards confirmed rejecting mid-pipeline and succeeding once terminal; `POST /:id/images` confirmed async (~3.1s response) with its added photos correctly analyzed in the background. **Not verified this session: the new frontend analysis view in an actual browser** — no browser-automation tool (e.g. Playwright MCP) was available this session, unlike prior phases; the frontend code was written and is included in this phase's diff, but only exercised indirectly via its backend calls. Test account, its reports, and its Storage objects deleted afterward. **Awaiting explicit instruction before starting Phase 6 or any further phase.**

Storage usage is honestly rendered as "Not yet available" — no byte-level tracking exists anywhere in this codebase (Golden Rule #1), confirmed via grep before deciding not to fabricate a number. New backend `GET /api/reports/dashboard-summary` endpoint. Found and fixed a real bug during live verification: the endpoint's Firestore `.aggregate(sum)` call needed a composite index that isn't provisioned in this project — replaced with a `.select()` projection summed in Node. Backend 64/64 tests, frontend 4/4, both lint-clean, both build. Fully live-verified in a real browser against a seeded populated test account and a genuinely empty new-user account (desktop+mobile, refresh persistence) — all metrics matched real Firestore data exactly; both test accounts deleted afterward. Root cause: `verifyIdToken()` transient failures were never made retryable (only a downstream Firestore-lookup failure was) — now retried once, then a 503 instead of a dead-end 401. ## Changelog (newest on top)

### [2026-08-20e] — Require + store claimant name/email before report generation, display in exports
- **Status:** DONE — backend 279/279 tests, frontend lint 0 errors, live-verified with a real headless-browser run through the report wizard plus direct API calls against the real dev backend, **UNCOMMITTED**.
- **Context:** Client audit found the app collects/requires `insuredName` (the claimant, not the account holder) but never `insuredEmail` anywhere in the report flow. Client clarified the required name/email belong to the claimant/insured and asked for a focused change: require + validate both before photo upload/generation, store them on the correct report, and show them in generated documents — no new document types, no unrelated changes.
- **What changed:** Backend `POST /api/reports/generate` (`reports.js`) now destructures `insuredEmail`, requires it alongside the existing `insuredName`/etc. (400 `VALIDATION_ERROR` if missing), validates email format (400 if malformed), applies the same 200-char limit as other identity fields, and persists it on the report doc; when a report is generated against a linked CRM claim, `insuredEmail` is auto-filled from the claim's client record (`client.email`) the same way `insuredName` already is from `client.name`. Added to `DUPLICATE_FIELDS` (carries over on report duplication) and to the shared HTML export template's meta grid. `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js` both gained an "Insured Email" row directly under "Insured Name" in the cover-page/header field list. Frontend: both wizards (`Dashboard.jsx`'s 5-step wizard and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s single-page form) gained a required "Insured Email" input next to "Insured Name" (with inline "Enter a valid email address" validation), and the existing insuredName-only gates were extended to also require a valid email — blocking the wizard's Step 1→Photos progression, the step-circle jump, and the final "Generate Report" button (mirrored in EnterpriseDashboard's photo-dropzone click and Generate button). Quick-demo templates got matching sample emails. Since agency/enterprise users can link a CRM claim instead of typing identity fields manually (hiding the manual inputs entirely), `ClaimLinkSection.jsx` was extended to resolve and pass through the linked client's email, auto-filling it, with a small editable "Insured Email" field shown in the linked-claim card for when the CRM client has no email on file — otherwise that flow would have had no way to satisfy the new required field.
- **Files touched:** `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js`, `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/components/ClaimLinkSection.jsx`.
- **QA done:** Backend: full `npm test` (279/279 pass, including the existing `exports.test.js`/`rich-export.test.js` PDF/DOCX/HTML snapshot-style assertions, unaffected). Frontend: `eslint` on all touched files (0 errors, only pre-existing unrelated warnings). Live-verified with a headless Playwright session against the real dev servers (backend :3000, frontend :5173): signed up a real test account (Firebase Admin used only to mark it email-verified and to delete it afterward — no manual-inbox step), drove the wizard, and confirmed the "Next" button is disabled with insuredEmail empty, stays disabled with an invalid value (inline error shown), and enables once a valid email is entered, successfully reaching the Photos step only then. Cross-checked server-side enforcement independently via direct authenticated API calls: missing `insuredEmail` → `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`; invalid format → `400`; valid → `200` with `insuredEmail` persisted on the created report doc. Confirmed the field renders in both exports by generating a real PDF and DOCX from a sample report object and extracting text/XML from each (`pdf-parse`, and unzipping the DOCX with `pizzip`) — both contain the "Insured Email" label and value. All test reports/users created during verification were deleted afterward.
- **Left / follow-ups:** EnterpriseDashboard's live browser walkthrough was not repeated separately (that dashboard only renders for agency/enterprise-tier accounts, and its gating code mirrors Dashboard.jsx's — verified by code review + lint, and by the shared backend enforcement already confirmed via direct API test). Not implemented (explicitly out of scope per the client's request): the missing document types identified in the earlier audit (Coverage Determination Letter, Invoice, Repair Estimate, Liability/Flood/Commercial/Mold report variants).
- **Golden-rule check:** none violated — no AI language, verdict, or entitlement logic touched; this only adds a required identity field to the existing human-entered claim-intake data.

### [2026-08-20d] — Logo click scrolls to top on Home, still navigates Home from elsewhere
- **Status:** DONE — lint 0 errors, live-browser verified headless (Playwright) on desktop (1280×800) and mobile (390×844) viewports, **UNCOMMITTED**.
- **Context:** Client wants clicking the FLACRONAI logo while already on the Home page to smoothly scroll back to the top instead of a full re-navigation to `/`, matching the pattern already used for hash-link nav items (`handleHashClick`). From any other page it must keep navigating to Home exactly as before.
- **What changed:** Added `handleLogoClick` in `Navbar.jsx`: when `location.pathname === '/'`, it `preventDefault()`s the `<Link to="/">`'s navigation, closes the mobile menu (`setMobileOpen(false)` — matches what the existing location-change effect would otherwise have done), and calls `window.scrollTo({ top: 0, behavior: 'smooth' })`. On any other route, the handler is a no-op and the `Link` navigates to `/` normally, unchanged from before. There's only one logo element in the component (shared by desktop and mobile — the mobile hamburger menu is a separate dropdown, not a duplicate logo), so one handler covers both.
- **Files touched:** `frontend/src/components/Navbar.jsx`.
- **QA done:** ESLint 0 errors. Started the Vite dev server and drove it with a headless Playwright script at both a desktop (1280×800) and mobile (390×844) viewport: (1) on `/`, scrolled to `y=1200`, clicked the logo, confirmed `scrollY` returned to `0` and the URL stayed `/` (no navigation/reload) — screenshot confirmed the hero section is visible at the top; (2) on `/pricing`, clicked the logo, confirmed the URL became `/` (normal navigation still works). No console errors in either viewport. Dev server stopped and the temporary test script removed afterward.
- **Left / follow-ups:** None.
- **Golden-rule check:** none violated — no claims, AI language, entitlements, or consent logic touched; purely a navbar interaction fix, scoped to the logo only.

### [2026-08-20] — Bugfix: Sample Report download form let users skip consent
- **Status:** DONE — frontend/backend lint clean, manual code-trace QA, **UNCOMMITTED**.
- **Context:** The Sample Report download modal (`LeadCaptureModal.jsx`) let users submit name/email and start the download without checking any consent box at all — the "Continue" button was never gated on it. Asked the client to clarify scope before changing behavior, since the modal's existing marketing-consent checkbox is explicitly worded as voluntary ("Consent is voluntary and I can withdraw it at any time") and the backend deliberately keeps that optional for downloads (Golden Rule #5) — making that specific checkbox mandatory would have contradicted its own wording. Client chose: add a separate, required "I agree to the Privacy Policy and Terms of Service" checkbox, keeping the existing marketing-consent checkbox untouched and still optional.
- **What changed:** Added a new `agreedToTerms` checkbox to `LeadCaptureModal.jsx`, unchecked by default, distinct from the pre-existing (still-optional) marketing-consent checkbox. The Continue/Download button is now `disabled` until it's checked, `handleSubmit` also blocks submission server-side-safe (in case of Enter-key submit bypassing the disabled button) with a toast + inline red validation message, and state resets on close/success like the rest of the form. Backend `POST /api/sales/contact` now rejects document-download submissions missing `termsAccepted: true` with `400 TERMS_REQUIRED`, mirroring the existing `CONSENT_REQUIRED` pattern already used for contact-form marketing consent — marketing consent for downloads remains fully optional and unaffected.
- **Files touched:** `frontend/src/components/LeadCaptureModal.jsx`, `backend/routes/sales.js`.
- **QA done:** Traced both request paths by hand: document-download source without `termsAccepted` → `400 TERMS_REQUIRED`; with it → passes through unchanged; non-download contact-form sources are untouched by the new check (still gated only by the pre-existing `CONSENT_REQUIRED` marketing-consent rule). Both changed files pass ESLint with 0 errors.
- **Left / follow-ups:** Live-browser click-through of the modal wasn't done this session (no connected browser) — worth a quick manual check that the button visibly enables/disables and the inline error renders correctly before this ships.
- **Golden-rule check:** Rule #5 preserved — the pre-existing marketing-consent checkbox stays optional, unchecked-by-default, and untouched; the new required checkbox is scoped to Privacy Policy/Terms of Service acknowledgment only, not marketing contact consent.

### [2026-08-15e] — Phase 5 (PHASES.md): Generate Report Wizard Completion
- **Status:** DONE — backend 72/72 tests (8 new), frontend 4/4, both packages lint/build clean, fully live-verified in a real browser incl. a real Anthropic API generation, **UNCOMMITTED**.
- **Context:** Client instructed implementing Phase 5 only, keeping the current 5-step wizard flow and adding the missing content without unnecessary restructuring (resolving `PHASES.md`'s open question in favor of the lower-risk option it already recommended).
- **Frontend implementation (`frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`):**
  1. **Step 1 (`ClaimIdentityFields`)** — added Policy Number, Insurance Company, Insured First Name/Last Name, Claim Type (`Property/Auto/Commercial/Liability/Other`), Property Type (`Single-Family Home/Multi-Family/Condo-Townhouse/Commercial/Other`). Insured Name remains the single canonical field every existing consumer depends on; First/Last are additive and live-compose into it.
  2. **Step 2 (Property Details)** — added structured Street/City/State/ZIP (same live-compose-into-the-canonical-field pattern as Insured Name), plus a new **Inspection Details** subsection: Inspection Date/Time, Inspector Name/ID, Inspection Type, Weather Conditions, Occupancy Status, Contact Present (+ conditional Contact Name).
  3. **Step 4 (renamed "Upload Photos & Documents")** — new Supporting Documents upload subsection: drag/drop or browse, PDF/DOC/DOCX/TXT up to 10MB/10 files, each shown with filename/formatted size/"Ready" status/remove button; invalid type or oversize rejected client-side with a toast before being staged.
  4. **Step 5 (Review & Generate)** — summary grid extended with Policy Number, Insurance Company, Claim Type, Property Type, Inspector, Inspection Date, Documents count.
  5. **Explicit "Save Draft" button** added to the wizard footer (all steps) alongside the pre-existing silent 30s autosave — same `localStorage` key, additionally shows a toast + a live "Saved HH:MM" label (updated silently by the autosave tick too). Photos/documents (File objects) still cannot survive `localStorage` — called out in the UI copy, an existing limitation, not new to this phase.
- **Backend implementation (`backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/config/storage.js`, new `backend/utils/documentValidation.js`):**
  1. `POST /generate`'s multer usage changed from `imageUpload.array('images', 100)` to a new combined `generateUpload` = `multer(...).fields([{images},{documents}])` instance, scoped to this one route only — the other two image-only routes (`/:id/images`, `/analyze-images`) are untouched and still receive `req.files` as a flat array.
  2. All 19 new fields (`policyNumber`, `insuranceCompany`, `insuredFirstName/LastName`, `claimType`, `propertyType`, `propertyStreet/City/State/Zip`, `inspectionDate/Time`, `inspectorName/Id`, `inspectionType`, `weatherConditions`, `occupancyStatus`, `contactPresent/Name`) are optional, validated when present (character limits mirroring the existing pattern, enum allowlists for the 5 select fields, date/time format checks), and persisted onto `reportDoc`. **Deliberately NOT added to `reportData`** (the object fed into `generateReport()`/`buildReportPrompt()`) — confirmed by reading `buildReportPrompt()`'s destructuring, which only ever pulls the specific fields it lists, so new keys on the input object are provably inert. This keeps "no AI-prompt change in this phase" true by construction, not just by intent.
  3. Documents are magic-byte validated by a new `documentValidation.js` (mirrors the existing `imageValidation.js` pattern: `%PDF-` for PDF, ZIP/OOXML signature for DOCX, OLE-compound-file signature for legacy DOC, a NUL-byte heuristic for `.txt`) before being persisted to `users/{uid}/reports/{reportId}/documents/{file}` (new `reportDocumentObject()` builder) via a new `persistReportDocuments()` helper, with metadata (`fileName/size/mimeType/objectPath/uploadedAt`) stored in a `documents` array on the report doc.
  4. New `GET /:id/documents/download` route retrieves a document by its stored `fileName`, mirroring the existing `/:id/download` export-proxy pattern.
- **`ReportDetailModal` extended** to show the new fields and a "Supporting Documents" list with working download links, using `report.field && [...]` guards throughout so a pre-existing (old-shape) report simply omits those rows instead of rendering blank/undefined values.
- **Real bug found and fixed during live verification:** the First/Last Name → Insured Name (and address-parts → Property Address) "auto-compose while empty" logic only checked whether the target field was empty before syncing — so typing First Name alone made Insured Name non-empty, which then permanently blocked Last Name from ever contributing (Insured Name froze at "Jane" instead of becoming "Jane Verifier"). Fixed by comparing against the *previous* composed value instead of only checking emptiness: the sync now keeps applying across multiple keystrokes/fields as long as the target still exactly equals what the parts would already produce, and only stops the moment the user types something else directly into it. Re-verified by reading the generated report's Firestore document directly: `insuredName: "Jane Verifier"`, `propertyAddress: "42 Verify Lane, Springfield, IL 62704"`.
- **Files created:** `backend/utils/documentValidation.js`, `backend/test/document-validation.test.js` (8 tests).
- **Files modified:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/config/storage.js`.
- **QA done:** `npm test` backend 72/72 passing (8 new, 0 regressions), frontend 4/4. `npm run lint` both packages 0 errors (38/15 warnings — the +1 backend warning is a `catch(err)`-unused pattern identical to the codebase's existing accepted convention in the neighboring `/:id/download` route). `npm run build` (frontend) succeeds. **Full live-browser verification** (Playwright, real dev servers tree-killed/restarted fresh multiple times — see rate-limiter finding below — real Firebase project, a throwaway QA account created and fully deleted afterward along with its Storage objects): complete wizard walkthrough with every new field filled, 1 photo + 2 documents uploaded (one removed to confirm remove works, verified via the "Ready" status badges and list count going 2→1), Save Draft clicked and confirmed via toast + "Saved HH:MM" label, a full page refresh mid-wizard confirmed text fields restored from `localStorage` exactly while photos/documents correctly reset to 0 (the documented, pre-existing files-can't-survive-localStorage limitation), the Review step showed every new field correctly, **Generate Report submitted successfully against the real Anthropic API** (`modelUsed: "anthropic/claude-opus-4-8"`) with the resulting Firestore document inspected directly to confirm every new field persisted exactly as entered including a fully-formed `documents` array; required-field validation confirmed disabling the Generate button with the existing warning text; uploading a `.jpg` through the document input confirmed rejected client-side (0 documents added); clicking a document's filename in `ReportDetailModal` confirmed triggering a real file download; a seeded pre-Phase-5 "old-shape" report (all 19 new fields entirely absent) confirmed opening in the report list and detail modal with zero crashes/console errors; desktop (1440×900) and mobile (390×844) both confirmed rendering every new section correctly, including on mobile with no layout breakage.
- **Significant pre-existing finding, NOT fixed (out of this phase's scope):** live testing repeatedly surfaced `429 Too Many Requests` on ordinary `GET /reports`/`/reports/templates`/`/reports/dashboard-summary` traffic as well as on `POST /generate` itself. Root cause: `backend/server.js`'s router-mounting table applies the AI-specific `aiLimiter` (**10 requests per 60 seconds**, intended for expensive AI-generation calls) as a `pre` middleware to the **entire** `/reports` router, not just AI/generation endpoints. `CLAUDE.md`'s existing fragility note about `GET /ai-status` sharing this bucket undersells the actual blast radius — a single Dashboard-home page load already fires ~4 requests into this same bucket (Phase 4 added 2 of them), so completely ordinary usage (open dashboard → browse My Reports → generate a report) can plausibly exhaust this budget within a minute in production, not just under automated test load. Worked around during this session's verification by restarting the backend between test passes (resets the in-memory counter); flagged here for a future dedicated fix, most likely scoping `aiLimiter` down to just `/generate`, `/analyze-images`, `/:id/images`, and `/:id/sections/suggest` instead of the whole router.
- **Golden-rule check:** No claims, entitlements, or consent logic touched. AI-prompt language and `buildReportPrompt()` untouched — verified by inspection that new fields never reach `reportData`. Document storage is private (no public token), matching the existing photo/export storage pattern (Golden Rule #6).
- **Next step:** Awaiting explicit instruction on which phase to implement next. Consider prioritizing a fix for the `aiLimiter` router-scoping finding above whenever convenient — it's a real production reliability risk independent of the phase roadmap's ordering.

### [2026-08-15d] — Follow-up: fixed the two Phase 4 flagged-but-not-fixed items
- **Status:** DONE — frontend lint/build clean, backend 64/64 tests unaffected (frontend-only change), live-verified in a real browser, **UNCOMMITTED**.
- **Context:** Client asked to fix the two pre-existing issues flagged (not fixed) in the Phase 4 write-up — the mobile FAB overlap and the stale hardcoded gradient hex — with the explicit instruction not to break any existing functionality ("koi pichli functionality khrab na ho").

This guarantees scrollable clearance below real content on every view (not just the new home view) so the fixed FAB always rests over blank space at full scroll, without touching the FAB's own position/behavior at all.

Live-verified with Playwright (fresh dev servers, real Firebase test account seeded with 2 reports then deleted after): mobile (390×844) screenshots at true bottom-of-scroll on both the Dashboard-home view and the pre-existing "My Reports" view confirm the FAB no longer overlaps real content on either; the sidebar gradient banner screenshot confirms the same diagonal orange-to-amber visual, now built from real brand tokens. ### [2026-08-15c] — Phase 4 (PHASES.md): Dashboard Home View
- **Status:** DONE — backend 64/64 tests, frontend 4/4, both packages lint/build clean, fully live-verified in a real browser, **UNCOMMITTED**.
- **Context:** Client instructed implementing Phase 4 only: `/dashboard` opened straight into the Generate Report wizard with no greeting, metrics overview, or FLACRON ENGINE usage panel — the missing home/overview screen from the spec audit (Item 18).
- **Implementation:**
  1. **`frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`** — default `activeView` changed from `'generate'` to a new `'home'` view (added as the first sidebar nav entry, icon `LayoutDashboard`). Greeting: `Good morning/afternoon/evening, {firstName}` — `firstName` reads `userProfile.firstName` (added in Phase 3) and falls back to splitting `displayName` for older accounts. 4 metric cards via a new local `MetricCard` component (loading skeleton / "Unavailable" error state / real value): Reports This Month (from the existing `userProfile.reportsThisMonth`/tier limit, not duplicated server-side), Photos Analyzed, Awaiting Review, Completed Reports (all three from the new backend endpoint below). Recent Reports table: own `fetchRecentReports`/`recentReports` state (deliberately separate from the "My Reports" tab's paginated/searchable state, to keep both simple), columns Claim #/Insured/Report Type/Photos/Status/Updated/Created By/Actions, reusing the existing `StatusBadge` and open-report interaction pattern; has its own loading-skeleton, error+Retry, and "No reports yet" + Generate Report CTA empty state. Flacron Engine Usage panel: photos processed + reports generated this month from real data, storage used explicitly rendered as **"Not yet available"** (not a fabricated number or silent 0 — Golden Rule #1). Primary "Generate Report" CTA in the header and inside the empty state.
  2. **`backend/routes/reports.js`** — new `GET /dashboard-summary` (`authenticateAny` + `reportsRead`), placed immediately after the existing `/ai-status` route and before any `/:id` route (matches the existing `/templates` route-ordering pattern, required so Express doesn't capture the literal path segment as an `:id` param). Returns `reportsAwaitingReview`/`reportsCompleted` via Firestore `.count()` (draft vs. `in ['finalized','completed','approved']`, the last two being legacy pre-Phase-2.7 statuses still possibly present on older reports) and `photosAnalyzed` via summing `imageCount` in Node. Deliberately minimal (3 numbers) — explicitly scoped to avoid duplicating Phase 15's future Analytics work.
  3. **`frontend/src/services/api.js`** — added `reportsAPI.getDashboardSummary()`.
- **Storage-usage decision (Golden Rule #1):** Grepped the entire codebase for `storageUsed`/`bytesUsed`/`totalStorage`/`storage_used` — zero matches anywhere, confirming no per-file/per-report byte-size accounting exists in Firestore or Storage-metadata reads. Rather than compute or estimate a number, the backend returns `storageAvailable: false` (no `storageUsed` field at all) and the frontend renders "Not yet available" in muted italic text with an explanatory tooltip.
- **Real bug found and fixed during live verification:** the endpoint's first implementation used Firestore's native `.aggregate({ totalPhotos: AggregateField.sum('imageCount') })` chained onto the `userId` equality filter. Live testing against the real Firebase project threw `9 FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index` — summing a field alongside an equality filter needs a Firestore composite index that isn't provisioned here (this repo has no `firestore.indexes.json`/`firebase.json` at all, so index management is ad hoc/console-driven, not CLI-deployed). Rather than take on a Firebase-console index-deployment step mid-phase, replaced the aggregate-sum with a `.select('imageCount').get()` projection scoped to the requesting user's own reports, summed in JS — avoids the composite-index dependency entirely while staying cheap (single field, one user's docs, not a full-collection scan). Re-verified clean after the fix.
- **Files modified:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`.
- **QA done:** `npm test` backend 64/64 passing (0 regressions — no new backend test added; endpoint is near-pure Firestore I/O and was validated via live data instead, see below), frontend 4/4. `npm run lint` both packages 0 errors (37/15 warnings, both identical to pre-existing baselines). `npm run build` (frontend) succeeds. **Full live-browser verification** (Playwright, real dev servers restarted fresh/tree-killed per the process-hygiene practice established in the Phase 3 session, real Firebase project): seeded a throwaway populated test account (5 reports: 2 draft, 2 finalized, 1 archived, `imageCount` totaling 48) and a separate genuinely-empty new-user account. Populated account: all 4 metric cards, the Recent Reports table (4 non-archived rows with correct claim #/insured/type/photos/status/dates), and the usage panel matched the seeded Firestore records exactly (Reports This Month 4/50, Photos Analyzed 48, Awaiting Review 2, Completed 2) at both desktop (1440×900) and mobile (390×844), with refresh persistence confirmed and zero console errors in a clean run. Empty account: all metrics correctly showed 0, Recent Reports showed the "No reports yet" empty state with CTA, usage panel showed 0/0 and "Not yet available" for storage, zero console errors in a clean run. The network-failure path (metric cards → "Unavailable", table → "We couldn't load your reports" + Retry) was incidentally exercised and confirmed working correctly, with no infinite skeletons or crashes. Both temporary QA accounts and their seeded report docs were deleted from Firestore after verification.
- **Test-harness artifact, not a Phase 4 bug:** running 4 account/viewport combinations back-to-back in rapid automated succession tripped the pre-existing global `express-rate-limit` (100 req/15min/IP, `backend/server.js`), producing intermittent 429s partway through the first full test pass. Confirmed as a test-harness effect (not a regression) by restarting the backend (resets the in-memory limiter) and re-running each scenario in isolation — zero 429s, zero console errors. Real single-user usage would not approach 100 requests in 15 minutes from this view.
- **Flagged but NOT fixed (pre-existing, out of Phase 4 scope):** (1) a mobile-only layout collision — the fixed-position sidebar-toggle FAB (`Dashboard.jsx` ~line 973, git-blamed to commit `b03eed6d`, 2026-07-31, predates this phase) visually overlaps whatever content scrolls into the bottom-left viewport region; on the new home view this happens to be the "Completed Reports" card label. This is a site-wide issue (any view's content can land under it while scrolling), not introduced by Phase 4 — my new layout just made it visible in this test pass. Flagged for a future dedicated mobile-layout pass rather than fixed silently, since a proper fix means auditing FAB clearance across every view. (2) A pre-existing hardcoded hex gradient on the sidebar profile card banner (`#f97316`/`#fb923c`/`#fbbf24`), a leftover from before Phase 2's brand-token cleanup — not touched, unrelated to this phase's scope.
